⏺ I scaffolded the project in
  /Users/jcolamendy/projects/interviews/parks-explorer-web with create-next-app,
  and npm run build passes.

  Stack
  - Next.js 16.3.8, using the App Router and Turbopack
  - React 19.2.8
  - Tailwind CSS v4, set up through @tailwindcss/postcss and
    src/app/globals.css, so there's no tailwind.config file
  - TypeScript and ESLint

  Layout
  - Source code is in src/, with the app in src/app/ (layout.tsx, page.tsx,
    globals.css).
  - The import alias is @/*, which points to src/*.
  - The generator also added AGENTS.md and CLAUDE.md.

  To start the dev server, run npm run dev and open http://localhost:3000.

  npm warned that unrs-resolver has an install script that isn't approved yet.
  Run npm approve-scripts --allow-scripts-pending if you want to review it. I
  haven't committed anything.

✻ Worked for 42s · done 4:22 PM

❯ /cg-interview-inline ### Requirements                                         
* Browse a list of parks                                                        
    * Display the supplied parks in a list,                                     
    * Show clear names and a way to select each park.                           
    * The list must work independently of the map - Should be able to find and  
select parks without using the map                                              
* Show park details                                                             
    * Show the selected park’s available name, address, description, amenities, 
hours, images, and any other useful supplied fields.                            
    * Do not invent values. Handle missing/null fields, empty arrays, and       
failed images gracefully.                                                       
    * Find a park in the list or map, select it, and read its details.          
* Show parks on a map                                                           
    * Display a marker for each park with valid coordinates. Show where parks   
are located.                                                                    
    * Start with a view that makes the plotted parks findable.                  
    * A park with no valid coordinates can still appear in the list and         
details—it simply cannot have a marker.                                         
    * Selecting a park should open its details or clearly link to them          
* Accessibility and layout:                                                     
    * Support the main tasks on phone and desktop. Stack layout for             
phone/mobile. Side-by-side layout for desktop                                   
    * Provide readable text, clear labels, keyboard-operated list-to-details    
navigation, and visible keyboard focus.                                         
    * Do not make map interaction the only way to get details.                  
* Location independence: Show parks without requesting or requiring the         
resident’s current location.                                                    
                                                                                
### Non-Requirements                                                            
                                                                                
Technical                                                                       
* Next.js / React / Tailwindcss                                                 
* React Leaflet + Leaflet                                                       
* Build ParkList, ParkDetails, ParkMap in its own component under               
src/components                                                                  
* Render React component on the client-side only (Next.js)                      
* Give the map container an explicit height using Tailwindcss                   
* For this local assessment, OpenStreetMap’s standard tiles avoid introducing   
an API key. Note in the README that a public deployment would need a reviewed   
tile-provider and usage plan                                                    
* Build around the supplied json                                                
* Build a README.md file                                                        
    * Install and run instructions                                              
    * Describing what works, decisions, omissions, known issues, verification,  
and important next steps before public use.                                     
                                                                                
Implementation Choices                                                          
* One selected-park ID should drive the list highlight, map selection, and      
detail panel.                                                                   
* Implement endpoint `/api/parks`                                               
    * read the supplied data and return it as JSON. Do not add a database, CRUD 
operations, authentication, or filtering `src/data/parks.sample.json`           
    * src/data/parks.sample.json                                                
                                                                                
#### Out of scope                                                               
* search, filters, ratings-based sorting, and geolocation                       
                                                                                
#### Acceptance Criteria                                                        
* List: Given the app loads, every usable park record appears in the list.      
Activating a list item shows details for that same park.                        
* Map: Given a park has valid latitude and longitude, its marker appears in an  
initial view that makes the plotted parks findable. Activating that marker      
shows or clearly links to that park’s details.                                  
* No coordinates: Given a park has missing or invalid coordinates, it remains   
selectable in the list and details, and the map does not render an invalid      
marker.                                                                         
* Sparse details: Given missing/null text, empty amenities/images arrays, or a  
failed image, details remain readable, do not crash, and do not claim facts the 
dataset does not provide.                                                       
* Keyboard: Starting outside the park list, use Tab to focus a park and         
Enter/Space as appropriate for the chosen control to open its details. The      
focus indicator is visible, and the new details are easy to locate and          
identify.                                                                       
* Responsive layout: At a narrow phone viewport and on desktop, a resident can  
find a park and read its details without content being clipped or controls      
becoming unusable.                                                              
* No location: On a fresh load with location permission unavailable, parks and  
details still work; the app does not request permission.                        
* Map failure: If map tiles fail to load, the list and details still function.  
* API, if retained: GET /api/parks returns the intended park data; if the       
request fails, the UI shows a useful error or retry path rather than an empty,  
unexplained screen.

⏺ I saved a working draft to docs/requirement/requirements.md. It has 8 user
  stories, 11 functional requirements and 10 acceptance criteria. I'll ask the
  open questions one at a time.

  Question (Q-001): src/data/parks.sample.json is referenced in your
  requirements but isn't in the repo. How should the requirements treat the data
  shape?

  1. Wait for the file (Recommended). Keep the requirements field-agnostic and
     pin the "usable record" and "valid coordinates" rules once the file
     arrives. Your brief says to build around the supplied JSON and not to
     invent values, so making up a fixture would break both.
  2. Create a labelled placeholder fixture now. This unblocks work, but the real
     file may have a different shape and cause rework.
  3. Define the expected schema in the requirements. This fixes the contract
     early, but it conflicts with "build around the supplied json".

  Which option do you want?

      104 +### Observed variability that drives behavior                 
      105 +                                                              
      106 +- **Address granularity varies:** full (`Riverside Dr, New Yor
          +k, NY 10024`), city-level only (`Brooklyn, NY 11225`), street 
          +only (`Ridge Rd`, `12 Old Mill Ln`), intersection (`44th St & 
          +7th Ave`), or absent. Display as supplied; never append a city
          +/state.

          +ht), `Dawn to dusk`, `24 hours`, and ranges. Display verbatim;
          + do not parse or show "open now".

          + the slug (replace hyphens, capitalise) without adding meaning
          +; unknown slugs must still render.

          + has images (10 of 12), so the fallback is exercised on every 
          +load and must look intentional.

⏺ I profiled src/data/parks.sample.json with a script and added a Data Contract
  section to docs/requirement/requirements.md. Q-001 is now resolved.

  What the data looks like

  - Shape: the file is a top-level array of 12 records, and /api/parks will
    return it unchanged.
  - Fields: every record has id, name, description, location {lat, lng, 
    address}, amenities[], hours, images[], acreage and rating. There are no
    other fields. There is no phone number, website, image alt text or sort
    field.
  - IDs and names: id values are unique kebab-case slugs, so they work as the
    selected-park ID. name values are also unique.
  - Gaps that need handling:
    - old-mill-botanical-garden has a null description.
    - highland-dog-park has no address key.
    - cedar-hill-nature-preserve and east-ridge-trailhead have empty images.
    - cedar-hill-nature-preserve also has a null rating.

  Findings that change the requirements

  1. All 12 sample parks have valid coordinates. The "no coordinates" behaviour
     can't be shown with the sample, so it needs test fixtures copied from it. I
     won't edit the sample file.
  2. Every image URL is on images.example.com, a placeholder domain that won't
     load. The failed-image fallback will therefore run on every load for 10 of
     the 12 parks, so it has to look deliberate. I added REQ-F-013 for this.
  3. Addresses are inconsistent. They range from a full street address to
     city-only, street-only, an intersection, or missing. They will be shown as
     supplied, with no city or state added.
  4. Hours are free text, such as 6:00 AM - 1:00 AM, Dawn to dusk and 24 hours.
     They will be shown verbatim, with no "open now" logic.
  5. Amenities are slugs like dog-run and kayak-launch. I allowed readable
     labels made only by formatting the slug.
  6. rating has no stated scale or review count, so showing "4.7/5" would claim
     something the data doesn't say.
  7. Cedar Hill's coordinates look wrong. cedar-hill-nature-preserve is at
     (40.7128, -74.0060), which is the commonly cited lower-Manhattan centre
     point, but it's described as protected woodland. I noted it as an
     observation; the app won't correct it.

  I also added REQ-F-012 (display rules), new edge cases and risks, and sample
  coverage notes on AC-003 and AC-004.

  Question (Q-002): What makes a park record "usable", meaning it must appear in
  the list? In the sample, all 12 have id, name and coordinates.

  1. Usable means a non-empty string id and a non-empty string name; everything 
     else is optional (Recommended). id is needed for the selected-park ID, and
     name is the list label. Coordinates shouldn't decide list membership,
     because your brief says parks without coordinates still appear.
  2. Usable means id, name and a location object. This is stricter, and it would
     drop records that lack a location, which contradicts your brief.
  3. Show every record, and fall back to the id when name is missing. Nothing is
     dropped, but it shows a label the dataset doesn't supply.

  Which option do you want?

  Question (Q-003): What counts as "valid coordinates" for drawing a marker? All
  sample values are finite numbers in the NYC area.

  1. lat and lng are both finite numbers, with lat in [-90, 90] and lng in 
     [-180, 180] (Recommended). Strings, null, NaN and out-of-range values are
     invalid. The rule is precise and easy to test, and it doesn't reject
     legitimate values like 0.
  2. Same as option 1, but (0, 0) is also invalid. This guards against a common
     "missing data" placeholder, but it rejects a point that is technically
     valid and doesn't appear in this dataset.
  3. Same as option 1, but numeric strings like "40.66" are converted and 
     accepted. This is more forgiving, but it quietly reinterprets data that the
     sample types as numbers.

  Which option do you want?

  Question (Q-004): Should the details panel show the supplied rating? The
  dataset gives no rating scale or review count, and ratings-based sorting is
  out of scope.

     the data never gives.

     leaves out a supplied field that your brief invites ("any other useful
     supplied fields").

  Question (Q-005): What should activating a map marker do?

  1. Select the park and update the details panel (Recommended). The shared
     selected-park ID updates the details panel, the list highlight and the
     marker state. There's no popup, so the details panel is the one place
     details appear. This matches your "one selected-park ID drives everything"
     rule.
  2. Select the park and also open a popup with the park name and a "View 
     details" link. This adds a cue on the map itself, but it duplicates content
     and adds a second keyboard and focus surface that has to be made
     accessible.
  3. Open a popup only, and select the park when its link is clicked. Your brief
     allows "clearly link to details", but selection happens after the click,
     and it weakens the shared-ID rule.

  Which option do you want?

  Question (Q-006): After a park is selected from the list or the map, how
  should the details be made easy to find? This matters most in the stacked
  phone layout, where the details may be off-screen, and for keyboard users.

  1. Move focus to the details heading and scroll it into view (Recommended).
     The heading takes programmatic focus (tabindex="-1"). One behaviour covers
     both the keyboard criterion and the phone layout, and screen readers
     announce the park name.
  2. Leave focus where it is, scroll the details into view, and announce the 
     change in an aria-live region. Focus stays on the list item for quick
     browsing. Keyboard users then have to Tab through the list to reach the
     details, and live-region announcements are less reliable.
  3. Move focus only on the phone layout. On desktop, where the details are
     already visible, focus stays on the list item. This avoids taking focus on
     desktop but behaves differently across screen sizes.

  Which option do you want?

  1. Nothing selected, with a neutral prompt (Recommended). The panel says
     something like "Select a park from the list or map to see its details", no
     park is highlighted, and the map starts fitted to all plotted parks. It
     shows only what the user chose, with no hidden default.
  2. Auto-select the first park in the array. The panel is never empty, but it
     implies a recommendation. It may also shift focus or scroll the page
     unexpectedly on load.
  3. Hide the details panel until a park is selected. The first screen is
     cleaner, but the layout shifts on the first selection and users can't see
     where the details will appear.

  Which option do you want?

✻ Brewed for 11s · done 4:30 PM

❯ option 1

⏺ cg-analysis Complete

  Requirements Extracted

  - Functional: 13 (REQ-F-001 to REQ-F-013)
  - Non-functional: 5 (REQ-NF-001 to REQ-NF-005)
  - All stable IDs are preserved, including the 5 constraints, 5 risks, 8
    stories and 10 acceptance criteria.

  Architectural Implications Mapped

  All 18 requirements and 5 constraints are mapped, along with the data,
  security, resilience and operations concerns and a test-level breakdown.

  Main decisions:
  - State: one client explorer component owns selectedParkId and the fetch state
    (loading, error, ready). The list, map and details are driven from it.
  - Pure helpers: normalizeParks applies the "usable" rule and
    hasValidCoordinates applies the valid-coordinates rule. List and map
    membership come from the same normalized list, so they can't disagree.
  - Map: ParkMap is loaded with next/dynamic({ ssr: false }) from a client
    component. Both the Next.js and React Leaflet docs confirm this is required.
    Leaflet CSS is imported once on the client.
  - Initial map view: MapContainer props can't change after the first render, so
    the initial bounds need a deliberate approach. The zero-marker and
    one-marker cases need fallbacks.
  - API: src/app/api/parks/route.ts returns the JSON file with no filtering.
    Validation happens on the client, and the client-side fetch is what makes
    the error and retry behaviour real.
  - Images: plain <img> instead of next/image, because the sample host is a
    non-resolving placeholder. Only http/https URLs are rendered, and every
    image has a fallback.
  - Accessibility: native <button> list items and a details heading with
    tabindex="-1" that takes focus only on selection, never on load.
  - Testing: unit, component, API and selective browser end-to-end tests.
    Browser tests are needed for Leaflet bounds, focus visibility, 375px and
    1280px layout, blocked tiles and the no-geolocation check. Performance
    testing isn't applicable.

  Open Questions

  Nothing blocks. Six choices are deferred to /cg-design:
  1. Test tooling, which also needs dev-dependency approval under AGENTS.md.
  2. Marker icon strategy: asset imports or a Tailwind-styled divIcon.
  3. Initial bounds approach.
  4. Breakpoint and the stacked order on phone.
  5. Whether to show a tile-failure notice.
  6. Confirm at install time that react-leaflet supports React 19.2.

  I also changed requirements.md: Q-002 to Q-007 still said Status: Open despite
  having recorded answers, so I set them to Resolved. That was my bookkeeping
  slip. No answers or requirement text changed.

  Written To

  - docs/analysis/requirements_analysis_parks-explorer-2026-10-02.md

  Human Quality Gate

  - Status: Pending
  - Required reviewers: product owner and technical owner. A security owner
    isn't required, because there's no auth, PII or secrets in scope.
  - Evidence: none yet.

  Next Step

  Please review the analysis and confirm approval, or tell me what to change.
  Once it's approved, I'll run /cg-design.

  Edge cases and constraints that also have no criterion:
  - Empty dataset, and zero or one plotted park: AC-002 only covers the case
    where several markers are plotted.
  - Excluded records: nothing tests the Q-002 rule that drops a record missing
    id or name, or a repeated id.
  - CON-002, CON-003 and CON-005: the OSM attribution, the src/components file
    layout and "no database" are never checked.

  My proposal is five new criteria:
  - AC-011 (REQ-F-002, REQ-F-012): a fully populated park shows every supplied
    field in the formats Q-004 and REQ-F-012 define.
  - AC-012 (REQ-F-007): marker selection highlights the marker and moves focus
    to the details heading.
  - AC-013 (REQ-NF-002): an automated accessibility check reports zero
    violations, every control has an accessible name, and text contrast is at
    least 4.5:1.
  - AC-014 (REQ-NF-004, REQ-NF-005): the production build succeeds, the map has
    a non-zero height at 375px and 1280px, and server render doesn't touch
    window.
  - AC-015 (edge cases, Q-002, CON-002/003/005): an empty dataset shows a
    distinct message, zero and one marker don't crash the map, bad or duplicate
    records are excluded, and the OSM attribution and component layout are
    present.

  I'd also add the missing requirement IDs to AC-004's tags. AC-009 bundles the

  or I can split it into two.

  add rows for the new criteria to the analysis.

  Should I add the five criteria and update the analysis?

⏺ The analysis gate is recorded as Approved for revision 9de8b75c23bc, and I
  confirmed the requirements file hasn't changed since. Preconditions are met,
  so I'm starting /cg-design at Step 0.

  ASSUMPTIONS I'M MAKING:

  Scope
  1. The design covers only what's in requirements.md: the list, details, map,
     shared selection, GET /api/parks, the responsive layout, accessibility and
     the README. It adds nothing from the out-of-scope list (search, filters,
     sorting, geolocation).
  2. This is a single-page app: one route (/) plus one API route. There's no
     routing between parks (for example no /parks/[id]), and the selected park
     lives in React state only, not in the URL.
  3. The /api/parks handler returns the JSON file untouched. All validation
     happens on the client in normalizeParks.

  Tech stack
  4. I'll use the installed Next.js 16.3.8, React 19.2.8, Tailwind v4 and
  TypeScript, adding only leaflet, react-leaflet and @types/leaflet at runtime.
  Their approval is a separate "ask first" item under AGENTS.md.
  5. Tests: Vitest with React Testing Library for unit, component and
  API-handler tests, and Playwright for browser tests. Playwright covers
  Leaflet, focus, viewport and tile-blocking checks, and @axe-core/playwright
  covers AC-013. None of this is installed yet, so it's a proposal to approve,
  not a fact.
  6. Markers use a Tailwind-styled divIcon rather than Leaflet's default image
  icons. This avoids the bundler icon-path problem and gives selected and
  unselected states without extra assets.
  7. The map is rendered only after data has loaded, so MapContainer's initial
  bounds are correct without a fitBounds child. The 0-marker and 1-marker cases
  get explicit fallbacks.

  Constraints and layout
  8. On phone the stacked order is list, then map, then details, with a scroll
  to the details heading after selection. On desktop the layout is two columns,
  with the list and details on one side and the map on the other, switching at
  Tailwind's lg (1024px) breakpoint. I'm treating both as design decisions, not
  requirements.
  9. Tile failures show a small non-blocking notice (an inline note under the
  map) rather than staying silent.
  10. No auth, database, caching layer, rate limiting, telemetry or CI config is
  introduced. The NFR checklist items about those will be marked N/A with a
  reason.
  11. The SDD file will be docs/design/design_spec_parks-explorer-2026-10-02.md,
  written section by section, and I'll ask for your approval after each
  section.

  → Correct me now, or I'll proceed with these.

  Also, do you have any questions before I proceed to Step 1 and 2 (loading
  context and the clarifying questions)?

⏺ User answered Claude's questions:
  ⎿  · What does your "yes" mean? → Assumptions confirmed, proceed

⏺ Assumptions confirmed. Step 1 is loading the project context, and I'll check
  the real state of the codebase and the dependency versions before proposing
  approaches.

  Read 1 file, ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ The research is done. Local facts I'll design against:

  - Scaffold: page.tsx and layout.tsx are create-next-app defaults, so this is
    greenfield with nothing to stay compatible with. The default next/image demo
    page and the "Create Next App" metadata get replaced.
  - Existing conventions: TypeScript is strict with the @/* alias, Tailwind v4
    is CSS-first, and the scaffold supports dark mode through
    prefers-color-scheme. Next 16 provides global LayoutProps<"/"> types.
  - React 19 compatibility (resolves D-06): react-leaflet@5.0.0 has peer
    dependencies react ^19, leaflet ^1.9, and the current Leaflet is 1.9.4, so
    there's no version conflict.
  - Test tooling: the current versions are Vitest 5.0.3, React Testing Library
    16.3.3, Playwright 1.63.0 and @axe-core/playwright 4.13.0. Vitest 5's
    @types/node peer is ^22 || >=24, while the project has ^20, so that peer
    will need a bump when it's installed.

  Three approaches

  A: Client explorer with pure helpers (Recommended)
  - A 'use client' ParkExplorer owns parks, selectedParkId and the fetch state.
  - It calls GET /api/parks, then runs the result through a pure normalizeParks.
  - ParkList, ParkDetails and ParkMap are presentational. ParkMap is loaded with
    dynamic(..., { ssr: false }) and rendered only once data is ready.
  - Tradeoffs: the error and retry path is real and testable, since the UI
    actually depends on the API (AC-009). The list isn't in the first
    server-rendered HTML, which doesn't matter for this app. Risk is low.

  B: Server Component reads the JSON directly
  - page.tsx imports the JSON on the server and passes it as props to the client
    UI.
  - Tradeoffs: the list is in the first server-rendered HTML and there's no
    loading state. But the UI never calls /api/parks, so AC-009's retry path
    can't be reached, and the endpoint exists only for show. Handling failure
    would mean error.tsx and a reload, which is a different behaviour from the
    requirement. Risk is medium, because it misses an acceptance criterion.

  C: A selection store synced to the URL (?park=id)
  - A context/reducer or small store holds the selection, mirrored to
    searchParams.
  - Tradeoffs: it gives shareable links and back-button behaviour. But nothing
    in the requirements asks for either, and it adds useSearchParams and
    Suspense handling. It also complicates the "no selection and no focus side
    effect on load" rule (Q-007), because a URL-driven selection on load must
    not steal focus. It's more moving parts for no required benefit, and it
    contradicts assumption 2. Risk is medium.

  I recommend A. It's the only option that satisfies AC-009 as written. The pure
  helpers also give cheap unit tests for the Q-002 and Q-003 rules and keep the
  list and map in agreement.

⏺ User answered Claude's questions:
  ⎿  · Which architecture approach should the SDD be written around? → A: Client
     explorer + pure helpers (Recommended)

⏺ Approach A is confirmed. I'll draft the SDD one section at a time and wait for
  your approval after each. Nothing is written to disk until a section is
  approved.

  ---

  Section 1 — Introduction & Purpose

  1.1 Background

  Residents have a supplied dataset of 12 parks (src/data/parks.sample.json) but
  no way to browse it, read details, or see locations. The repository is a
  fresh create-next-app scaffold (Next.js 16.3.8, React 19.2.8, Tailwind v4,
  TypeScript strict) with no feature code. This SDD describes how a single-page
  Next.js app will satisfy the approved requirements using Approach A: a
  client-side explorer that fetches GET /api/parks, normalizes the data with
  pure helpers, and drives the list, map, and details from one selectedParkId.

  1.2 Scope

  - One page (/) with ParkList, ParkMap, and ParkDetails under src/components.
  - One Route Handler, GET /api/parks, serving the supplied JSON unmodified.
  - Pure data helpers (usable-record filter, coordinate validation, display
    formatting).
  - Responsive stacked and side-by-side layout, keyboard and screen-reader
    support, and failure handling for API, tiles, and images.
  - A README.md replacing the scaffold's, and the test strategy that proves each
    acceptance criterion.

  1.3 Out of Scope

  Search, filters, ratings-based sorting, geolocation, database, CRUD,
  authentication, server-side filtering, URL-synced selection, caching layers,
  telemetry, and CI configuration. Public-deployment concerns (tile provider,
  image hosting) are documented in the README, not built.

  1.4 Requirements Baseline

  - Requirements: docs/requirement/requirements.md, sha256 9de8b75c23bc
    (REQ-F-001 to 013, REQ-NF-001 to 005, CON-001 to 005, AC-001 to 015, Q-001
    to Q-007 resolved).
  - Analysis: docs/analysis/requirements_analysis_parks-explorer-2026-10-02.md,
    gate Approved for that revision (2026-10-02 16:36).
  - Stable IDs are preserved and never restated here as prose; Section 11 maps
    every ID to a DES-* item.
  - Design-deferred items from the analysis (D-01 to D-06) are resolved in
    Sections 2, 7, and 8. D-06 is already answered: react-leaflet@5.0.0 supports
    React 19.

  Does this look right so far? If yes, I'll continue to Section 2 (System
  Architecture).

⏺ Section 2 — System Architecture

  2.1 High-Level Architecture

  mermaid
  flowchart LR
      subgraph Server
          JSON[(src/data/parks.sample.json)]
          API["DES-006 GET /api/parks"]
          PAGE["page.tsx (Server Component)"]
          JSON --> API
      end
      subgraph Browser
          EXP["DES-001 ParkExplorer<br/>state: parks, selectedParkId, status"]
          LIB["DES-002 lib/parks (pure)<br/>normalizeParks, hasValidCoordinates,
  formatters"]
          LIST["DES-003 ParkList"]
          DET["DES-004 ParkDetails + ParkImage"]
          MAP["DES-005 ParkMap (client-only)"]
      end
      OSM[(OpenStreetMap tiles)]
      IMG[(Image hosts)]
      PAGE --> EXP
      EXP -->|fetch| API
      EXP --> LIB
      EXP --> LIST
      EXP --> DET
      EXP -->|dynamic ssr:false| MAP
      MAP --> OSM
      DET --> IMG

  2.2 Components

  - DES-001 ParkExplorer ('use client'): sole owner of parks, selectedParkId,
    and fetch status (loading | error | ready). Fetches, normalizes, renders
    layout, and moves focus to the details heading on selection.
  - DES-002 src/lib/parks.ts: pure, DOM-free logic: types, normalizeParks,
    hasValidCoordinates, getMarkerParks, display formatters.
  - DES-003 ParkList: list of <button> items; reports selection upward.
  - DES-004 ParkDetails: renders the selected park or the neutral prompt; owns
    image fallback.
  - DES-005 ParkMap: Leaflet map, one divIcon marker per valid park, bounds
    fitting, tile-failure notice. Loaded via next/dynamic with ssr: false from
    DES-001.
  - DES-006 Route Handler src/app/api/parks/route.ts: returns the JSON file
    unchanged.

  2.3 Interfaces

  Interface: GET /api/parks
  Producer → consumer: DES-006 → DES-001
  Contract: HTTPS JSON array of park records
  Auth / versioning: none / none
  Failure semantics: Non-2xx, network error, invalid JSON, or 10 s timeout →
  error state with Retry (user-initiated only)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Interface: Component props
  Producer → consumer: DES-001 → children
  Contract: typed props (parks, selectedParkId, onSelect)
  Auth / versioning: n/a
  Failure semantics: Unknown selectedParkId → treated as no selection
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Interface: OSM tiles
  Producer → consumer: OSM → DES-005
  Contract: {s}.tile.openstreetmap.org/{z}/{x}/{y}.png
  Auth / versioning: none; attribution required
  Failure semantics: Tile errors affect the map only; non-blocking notice
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Interface: Image URLs
  Producer → consumer: data hosts → DES-004
  Contract: http(s) URLs only
  Auth / versioning: none
  Failure semantics: error event → fallback block

  No events, storage, or operator interfaces exist. Ownership: this repository
  owns everything except OSM and image hosts.

  2.4 Architecture Principles

  1. One source of truth: selectedParkId lives only in DES-001.
  2. Pure core, thin shell: all rules live in DES-002 and are unit-testable.
  3. The map is progressive enhancement; list and details never depend on it.
  4. Render only supplied facts; treat data as untrusted input.

  Does this look right so far?

  2.2 Components and file placement

  ┌─────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │     Folder      │                        Contents                        │
  ├─────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │                 │ page.tsx (renders ParkExplorer), layout.tsx,           │
  │ src/app/        │ globals.css, api/parks/route.ts (DES-006). Required by │
  │                 │  Next.js routing.                                      │
  ├─────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤

  │                 │ ParkImage (004), ParkMap (005).                        │
  ├─────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │                 │ ParksContext.tsx (DES-007): provider holding parks,    │
  │ src/contexts/   │ status, selectedParkId, selectPark, retry. The single  │
  │                 │ source of truth.                                       │
  ├─────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤

  │                 │ selection change, never on mount).                     │
  ├─────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │                 │ ExplorerLayout.tsx (DES-010): responsive shell,        │
  │ src/layouts/    │ stacked below lg and two columns at lg and above,      │
  │                 │ taking list, map, and details as slots.                │
  ├─────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │                 │ DES-002: types.ts, parks.ts (normalizeParks,           │
  │ src/lib/        │ hasValidCoordinates, getMarkerParks), format.ts        │
  │                 │ (amenity, acres, rating strings), api.ts (fetchParks). │
  │                 │  All DOM-free.                                         │
  ├─────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ src/data/       │ parks.sample.json (exists; never edited).              │
  ├─────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ src/assets/     │ Only the image-fallback icon (SVG), if one is needed.  │
  │                 │ Left empty otherwise.                                  │
  └─────────────────┴────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  Tests sit beside the source as *.test.ts(x). Browser tests go in a root-level
  e2e/ folder, outside src/, so src/ keeps the mandated structure.

  2.3 Interfaces

  Unchanged from the approved draft, except the fetch is now owned by
  useParksFetch (DES-008) and consumers read state through useParks(), not
  props. The /api/parks contract, OSM tile and image failure semantics, and the
  10 s timeout stay the same.

  2.4 Architecture Principles

  1. One source of truth: selectedParkId lives only in ParksContext.
  2. Pure core, thin shell: all rules in lib/, side effects only in hooks and
     the context.
  3. The map is progressive enhancement; list and details never depend on it.
  4. Render only supplied facts; treat data as untrusted input.
  5. Code lives in the mandated src/ folders; no new top-level folders without
     approval.

  Two points to confirm:
  - Tests beside source plus a root e2e/ folder. Is that acceptable, or should
    the Playwright tests live elsewhere?
  - src/assets/ stays empty unless the image fallback needs an icon. Is that OK?

  Does this revised Section 2 look right so far?

⏺ Section 3 — Workflow / Use Cases

  3.1 DES-011 Initial load

  mermaid
  sequenceDiagram
      actor R as Resident
      participant P as ParksProvider (DES-007)
      participant F as useParksFetch (DES-008)
      participant A as GET /api/parks (DES-006)
      participant L as lib/normalizeParks (DES-002)
      R->>P: open /
      P->>F: mount, status = loading
      F->>A: fetch (10 s timeout)
      alt 200 + valid JSON
          A-->>F: raw array
          F->>L: normalizeParks(raw)
          L-->>F: usable parks
          F-->>P: status = ready, parks
          Note over P: list renders, map mounts, details show prompt, nothing
  selected, no focus change
      else failure / timeout / invalid JSON
          F-->>P: status = error
          Note over P: error message + Retry button
      end

  An empty result after normalization is ready with zero parks and shows a
  distinct "no parks available" message (AC-015a).

  3.2 DES-012 Selecting a park (list or marker)

  mermaid
  sequenceDiagram
      actor R as Resident
      participant S as ParkList or ParkMap
      participant P as ParksProvider
      participant D as ParkDetails
      R->>S: click / Enter / Space (list button or marker)
      S->>P: selectPark(id)
      P-->>S: selectedParkId = id (list highlight, marker selected style)
      P-->>D: render park
      D->>D: useFocusOnSelect: focus heading, scroll into view

  selectPark also increments a focus-request counter, so re-activating the
  already selected park still returns focus to the details heading. An id not
  found in parks shows the neutral prompt.

  3.3 DES-013 Failure and degradation paths

  - API failure: error view, Retry re-runs DES-008; no automatic retry.
  - Tile failure: non-blocking notice under the map; markers, list, and details
    keep working.
  - Image failure: per-image fallback block in ParkImage; other images
    unaffected.
  - No valid coordinates: park stays in list and details; excluded from markers.
    With zero marker parks the map shows a default view and no markers; with
    one, it centres on it.
  - Geolocation: never called anywhere (AC-007).

  Does this look right so far?

⏺ Section 4 — Data Models

  There is no persistence layer. The models are TypeScript types in
  src/lib/types.ts, plus the normalization contract that turns the raw JSON into
  them.

  4.1 DES-014 Raw record and normalized Park

  The API and the sample file carry the raw shape (12 records today, top-level
  array). The client never trusts it: normalizeParks(raw: unknown) produces
  Park[].

  ┌─────────────┬──────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │ Park field  │     Type     │             Normalization rule             │
  ├─────────────┼──────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ id          │ string       │ Required, trimmed, non-empty. First        │
  │             │              │ occurrence wins on duplicates.             │
  ├─────────────┼──────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ name        │ string       │ Required, trimmed, non-empty.              │
  ├─────────────┼──────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ description │ string |     │ Non-string, empty, or whitespace-only →    │
  │             │ null         │ null.                                      │
  ├─────────────┼──────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ address     │ string |     │ From location.address, same rule as        │
  │             │ null         │ description.                               │
  ├─────────────┼──────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │             │ { lat:       │ Non-null only if both are finite numbers,  │
  │ coordinates │ number; lng: │ lat in [-90, 90], lng in [-180, 180]       │
  │             │  number } |  │ (Q-003). Derived field: markers are        │
  │             │ null         │ exactly the parks where it is non-null.    │
  ├─────────────┼──────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ amenities   │ string[]     │ Non-array → []; non-string or empty        │
  │             │              │ entries dropped.                           │
  ├─────────────┼──────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ hours       │ string |     │ Same text rule; shown verbatim.            │
  │             │ null         │                                            │
  ├─────────────┼──────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ images      │ string[]     │ Non-array → []; only http: and https: URLs │
  │             │              │  kept.                                     │
  ├─────────────┼──────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ acreage     │ number |     │ Finite and ≥ 0, else null.                 │
  │             │ null         │                                            │
  ├─────────────┼──────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ rating      │ number |     │ Finite number, else null.                  │
  │             │ null         │                                            │
  └─────────────┴──────────────┴────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  Unknown extra fields are dropped, not rendered. Records missing a usable id or
  name are excluded (Q-002, AC-015c). I treat whitespace-only values as empty,
  since a blank label would be unusable; this is a design clarification of
  "non-empty", not a requirement change.

  4.2 DES-015 Provider state (ParksContext)

  ┌────────────────┬────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────┐
  │     Field      │        Type        │               Notes               │
  ├────────────────┼────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────┤
  │ status         │ 'loading' |        │ Error carries a user-safe         │
  │                │ 'error' | 'ready'  │ message.                          │
  ├────────────────┼────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────┤
  │ parks          │ Park[]             │ Empty until ready; may be empty   │
  │                │                    │ after.                            │
  ├────────────────┼────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────┤
  │ selectedParkId │ string | null      │ null on first load (Q-007).       │
  ├────────────────┼────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────┤
  │ focusRequest   │ number             │ Incremented on every selectPark,  │
  │                │                    │ so reselection refocuses.         │
  └────────────────┴────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────────┘

  Relationships: selectedPark is derived by id lookup (never stored); marker
  parks are derived from coordinates !== null, so list and map cannot disagree.

  Does this look right so far?

⏺ Section 5 — API Endpoints

  There is one endpoint, plus the client call that consumes it.

  5.1 DES-016 GET /api/parks

  ┌─────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │   Aspect    │                          Design                           │
  ├─────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ File        │ src/app/api/parks/route.ts (Route Handler, GET only)      │
  ├─────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Request     │ No params, body, or auth                                  │
  ├─────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │             │ 200, Content-Type: application/json, body = the contents  │
  │ Success     │ of src/data/parks.sample.json unmodified (a top-level     │
  │             │ array; 5,910 bytes today)                                 │
  ├─────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Data access │ Static import of the JSON, so no runtime file-system      │
  │             │ access and no user-controlled path                        │
  ├─────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Validation  │ None on the server: the body must deep-equal the file     │
  │             │ (AC-009); validation happens in the client (DES-002)      │
  ├─────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Auth /      │ None / none (local assessment, one consumer)              │
  │ versioning  │                                                           │
  ├─────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Other       │ Not exported, so Next.js answers 405                      │
  │ methods     │                                                           │
  ├─────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │             │ Handler has no catch. Any unexpected failure is Next's    │
  │ Errors      │ default 500, with no stack trace in production. The UI    │
  │             │ treats every non-2xx the same way.                        │
  ├─────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Caching     │ Framework defaults; no explicit cache headers (small,     │
  │             │ static payload)                                           │
  └─────────────┴───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  Request and response shapes are the raw record shape documented in the
  requirements Data Contract. No other endpoints exist, and none for CRUD or
  filtering.

  5.2 DES-017 Client call (lib/api.ts → fetchParks)

  Used only by useParksFetch (DES-008).
  - GET /api/parks with cache: "no-store", so Retry always re-requests.
  - An AbortController aborts after 10 s.
  - Success requires res.ok, parseable JSON, and an array.
  - It throws on network error, timeout, non-2xx, invalid JSON, or non-array;
    useParksFetch turns every throw into status = 'error' with one user-safe
    message (no raw error text shown).
  - Retry is user-initiated only: no automatic retry, no stacked retries, no
    backoff.

  Does this look right so far?

⏺ Section 6 — Non-functional Requirements

  Anything the checklist covers but this app doesn't need is marked N/A with a
  reason, not skipped silently.

  Area: Performance (DES-018)
  Design decision: The requirements set no latency or throughput target, so none

  is invented. The budget is practical: 12 records, a 5.9 KB payload, no
  pagination (the dataset is fixed and small; the README lists this as a
  limit). ParkMap is code-split via dynamic, so the list renders without
  loading Leaflet. Images use loading="lazy". Load, stress, and soak tests:
  N/A.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Area: Security (DES-019)
  Design decision: No auth, PII, secrets, or user input. Controls: image URLs
  limited to http(s) (DES-014); React text rendering only, never
  dangerouslySetInnerHTML; referrerPolicy="no-referrer" on images; static JSON
  import, so no path traversal. New runtime dependencies (leaflet,
  react-leaflet) and dev tools need review approval per AGENTS.md. CSP, CORS,
  HSTS, and rate limiting: N/A for this local assessment, listed in the README
  as pre-public work.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Area: Consistency
  Design decision: N/A: read-only static data, one client state owner (DES-007).

  The one rule: re-activating the selected park still refocuses the details
  (DES-012).
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Area: Availability
  Design decision: No SLO, so none is invented. Degradation ladder (DES-013):
  tiles fail → list and details work; API fails → error plus Retry; image fails
   → fallback block. Multi-region, failover, and backups: N/A.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Area: Scalability / Cost
  Design decision: N/A for 12 records and zero infrastructure. OSM's public
  tiles
  are free but not suitable for public traffic (RISK-002), so the README
  requires a reviewed provider plan before public use.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Area: Reliability
  Design decision: Every failure class maps to one UI state (DES-013). Timeouts:

  10 s on the fetch (DES-017). Edge cases (empty data, 0 or 1 marker, bad or
  duplicate records, long text) are handled by DES-014 and AC-015, with long
  strings wrapping (break-words, min-w-0) to prevent horizontal scroll.
  Retries, circuit breakers, and dead-letter queues: N/A.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Area: Observability (DES-022)
  Design decision: No metrics, tracing, or alerting. A single console.error in
  the fetch-failure path only.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Area: Accessibility and layout (DES-020)
  Design decision: List items are <button>s in a <ul>, with aria-current on the
  selected one. The details region is labelled, its heading focusable
  (tabindex="-1"). Controls use focus-visible: rings. Text contrast ≥ 4.5:1,
  and scrolling honours prefers-reduced-motion. Layout: stacked below lg, two
  columns at lg and above. The map wrapper has an explicit Tailwind height
  (REQ-NF-005).
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Area: Theme (DES-021)
  Design decision: Proposed: light theme only. OSM tiles are always light, and
  AC-013 would otherwise need contrast verified in two themes. This removes the
   scaffold's prefers-color-scheme: dark block and sets color-scheme: light.

  One decision for you (DES-021): do you want light-only as proposed? The
  alternative is keeping dark mode, which means verifying AC-013 in both themes
  and accepting a bright map inside a dark page.

  Reply yes to approve Section 6 as written, light-only included, or tell me
  what to change.

⏺ Section 8 — Design Decisions & Tradeoffs

  The three analysis items still open (D-01 tooling, D-02 marker icons, D-03
  initial view) plus the layout, tile-notice and image decisions are settled
  here. D-06 is already answered: react-leaflet@5.0.0 supports React 19.

  ID: DES-024 (D-01)
  Decision: Test tooling
  Options considered: (a) Vitest + React Testing Library + Playwright +
  @axe-core/playwright; (b) Jest + RTL + Cypress; (c) Playwright only
  Recommendation and rationale: (a). Vitest is fast with TypeScript and ESM and
  has no Babel setup. Playwright covers the real-browser criteria (Leaflet
  bounds, focus, viewports, blocked tiles) and has first-class request
  interception. axe gives AC-013 a measurable result.
  Tradeoff: Four new dev dependencies needing approval. Vitest 5 asks for
  @types/node ^22 or ≥24 while the project has ^20, so that devDependency gets
  bumped. (c) is smaller but makes the pure-logic tests slow and coarse.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ID: DES-025 (D-02)
  Decision: Marker icons
  Options considered: (a) Leaflet default image icons with explicit asset
  imports; (b) Tailwind-styled divIcon
  Recommendation and rationale: (b). Avoids the known bundler icon-path problem,

  needs no assets, and gives selected and unselected styling directly.
  Tradeoff: Custom markup to maintain. Accessible names and keyboard activation
  are verified during implementation (AC-013, AC-012), not assumed.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ID: DES-026 (D-03)
  Decision: Initial map view
  Options considered: (a) Mount the map after data is ready and pass bounds; (b)

  always mount and call fitBounds from a child
  Recommendation and rationale: (a). MapContainer's bounds are fixed after first

  render, and data is fixed after ready, so (a) is simpler and needs no effect.
   Zero marker parks → world view ([20, 0], zoom 2, which asserts no location);
   one → centred on it at zoom 14.
  Tradeoff: The map isn't mounted while loading or in error, so a same-height
  placeholder prevents layout shift.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ID: DES-027 (D-04)
  Decision: Layout
  Options considered: (a) lg (1024 px) breakpoint, stacked order list → map →
  details; (b) md; map first
  Recommendation and rationale: (a). 375 px and 1280 px, the widths the ACs use,

  sit clearly on either side of lg. Selection scrolls to the details heading,
  so a details panel below the map is still reachable. Desktop puts list and
  details in one column and the map in the other.
  Tradeoff: Details are far from the list on phone. The scroll and focus
  behaviour compensates.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ID: DES-028 (D-05)
  Decision: Tile-failure feedback
  Options considered: (a) Silent; (b) one non-blocking role="status" note
  Recommendation and rationale: (b). Residents otherwise see a blank map with no

  explanation. It is set once, not per tile, since tileerror fires for every
  tile.
  Tradeoff: One more element to test.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ID: DES-029
  Decision: Image element
  Options considered: (a) next/image; (b) plain <img>
  Recommendation and rationale: (b). next/image needs remotePatterns for each
  host, and the sample host is a non-resolving placeholder. A plain <img> also
  fires error for the fallback.
  Tradeoff: No automatic image optimisation, acceptable for this scope.

  Decisions already approved in earlier sections: Approach A (client explorer),
  Context for shared state (the mandated contexts/ folder), no marker popup
  (Q-005), light theme only (DES-021).

  Does this look right so far?

❯ Change the following decision:                                                
  - Vitest + React Testing Library. No playwright. I will do end-to-end         
  manually                                                                      

⏺ Understood: Vitest and React Testing Library only, with no Playwright, and you
  run the end-to-end checks by hand. That change reaches into Sections 2, 7 and
  8, and some criteria can't be fully proven in jsdom, so here is what changes.

  Revised DES-024 (Section 8)

  Decision: Test tooling
  Recommendation and rationale: Vitest + React Testing Library (with user-event,

  jest-dom, jsdom, @vitejs/plugin-react), plus vitest-axe for structural
  accessibility rules (to be verified at install). Playwright and
  @axe-core/playwright are dropped.
  Tradeoff: Fewer dependencies, but jsdom has no layout, no real Leaflet
  viewport, and no computed contrast. Anything that needs a real browser is a
  manual end-to-end check you run. @types/node still needs the bump to ^22 or
  ≥24 for Vitest 5.

  What jsdom can't prove, and how it's covered

  ┌──────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │   Needs a real browser   │                  Covered by                  │
  ├──────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────┤

⏺ Section 9 — Algorithms (DES-030)

  Three small algorithms are worth pinning down because their edge cases are
  where the acceptance criteria bite. All are O(n) over at most a few dozen
  parks.

  9.1 normalizeParks(raw)

  1. If raw isn't an array → treat as empty.
  2. For each entry that is an object, trim id and name. Skip the entry if
     either is missing or empty.
  3. Skip the entry if its id was already seen (first wins).
  4. Build the Park per the DES-014 table; derive coordinates only when both
     values pass the Q-003 range check.
  5. Keep input order, since the dataset supplies no other order.

  Assessment: a single pass with a Set of seen IDs. It never throws on malformed
  input, which is what keeps one bad record from blanking the screen.

  9.2 Initial map view (DES-026)

  - 0 marker parks: world view, center [20, 0], zoom 2.
  - 1 marker park, or ≥ 2 whose coordinates are all identical: centre on it at
    zoom 14. Identical points would otherwise give a zero-area bounds.
  - ≥ 2 distinct points: bounds of min/max lat and lng, with padding [32, 32] so
    edge markers aren't clipped.

  Assessment: this covers the cases AC-002 and AC-015(b) test. Leaflet's own
  fitBounds behaviour on degenerate bounds is the reason for the special case.

  9.3 Focus on selection (DES-009)

  Selection increments focusRequest. The effect runs only when focusRequest > 0
  (so never on first load, Q-007): it focuses the details heading, then scrolls
  it into view with behavior: "smooth", or "auto" if prefers-reduced-motion is
  set.

  Assessment: a counter, not the selected ID, drives the effect, so
  re-activating the same park refocuses. The risk is a user who has just moved
  focus elsewhere being pulled back, and that's the intended behaviour (Q-006).

  9.4 Amenity label

  Split the slug on -, join with spaces, uppercase the first character (dog-run
  → "Dog run"). Entries that come out empty are dropped. Unknown slugs still
  render. No synonyms or translations, so nothing is added to the data.

  Does this look right so far?

✻ Crunched for 11s · done 4:48 PM

❯ yes                                                                           

⏺ Section 10 — Boundaries

  Always Do

  - Validate every record at the boundary with normalizeParks; never render raw
    API data.
  - Write the failing test first, then the code (TDD, per AGENTS.md).
  - Show only supplied facts: verbatim hours and address, "Rating: {n}" with no
    scale, "Rating not provided" when null.
  - Keep list, map and details driven by the one selectedParkId in ParksContext.
  - Keep functions at 70 lines or fewer, and place code only in the mandated
    src/ folders.
  - Run npm test, npm run lint and npm run build before claiming a phase done,
    and report the exact output.
  - Make one atomic commit per phase once checks pass.
  - Record the manual e2e results (date, pass/fail) in the README Verification
    section.

  Ask First

  - Adding or upgrading any runtime or dev dependency (leaflet, react-leaflet,
    the Vitest stack, and the @types/node bump all need approval).
  - Any change to the /api/parks contract, or to the requirements, analysis or
    SDD (these reset their approval gates).
  - Adding a folder under src/ beyond the mandated set, or moving the manual e2e
    checklist out of the README.
  - Switching the tile provider or adding any API key or environment variable.
  - Adding any feature from the out-of-scope list (search, filters, sorting,
    geolocation, URL-synced selection).
  - Changing CI, build or lint configuration.

  Never Do

  - Edit src/data/parks.sample.json; test fixtures clone records in code.
  - Call navigator.geolocation or any location API.
  - Invent, infer or "fix" data: no added city or state, no "/5", no corrected
    coordinates, no open/closed status.
  - Use dangerouslySetInnerHTML, or render a non-http(s) image URL.
  - Show stack traces or raw error text to users.
  - Weaken, skip or delete a test just to make a gate pass.
  - Commit secrets, or git push without an explicit request.
  - Touch docs/policy/engineering_constitution.md outside a dedicated, approved
    policy change.

  Does this look right so far?

⏺ Adversarial review: findings before any edits

  I read the saved SDD against the real scaffold (layout.tsx, page.tsx,
  globals.css, tsconfig, ESLint) and the current Leaflet docs. I ran four
  rounds. By round 4 the critiques were mostly niche, so I stopped there.

  One caveat on sources: the Leaflet docs I could reach describe the main branch
  (Leaflet 2.x-style imports), but the project would install 1.9.4. I treat
  marker behaviour as "verify against the installed version", not as proven.

  Round 1 — logic and requirements coverage

  ID: F1
  Problem: useParksFetch has no cancellation. React StrictMode double-runs
  effects in dev, and Retry or unmount can leave a stale response overwriting
  newer state.
  Proposed fix: Abort the request on cleanup, and ignore any response that isn't

  the latest request.
  Risk: Medium
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ID: F2
  Problem: Desktop layout (list and details in one column, map in the other)
  with
  DOM order list → map → details means tab order doesn't match visual order. A
  WCAG focus-order problem.
  Proposed fix: Decision needed, see below.
  Risk: Medium
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ID: F3
  Problem: The SDD never specifies the page shell. There's no <h1>, no
  <main>/landmarks, and the scaffold's "Create Next App" title and metadata
  would ship.
  Proposed fix: Add to DES-020: update layout.tsx metadata, one <h1>, <main>,
  and
  labelled <section>s for list, map and details.
  Risk: Medium

  Round 2 — UI, accessibility and library behaviour

  ID: F4
  Problem: The Leaflet docs confirm keyboard: true makes a marker tabindex=0
  with
  role=button, and say each marker needs a unique alt or title. They don't
  confirm Space activates it, and for a divIcon the name has to come from
  custom HTML.
  Proposed fix: Give each marker a title plus visually hidden park-name text in
  the icon HTML. Verify Enter and Space on 1.9.4. Add a small key handler for
  Space if it's missing.
  Risk: High
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ID: F5
  Problem: scrollIntoView to the top of the heading on every selection makes the

  desktop page jump even when details are already visible.
  Proposed fix: Skip the scroll when the heading is fully in the viewport. This
  still meets AC-012.
  Risk: Low
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ID: F6
  Problem: The selected list item and selected marker may be distinguished by
  colour alone (WCAG 1.4.1).
  Proposed fix: Add a non-colour cue: border/weight on the list item, size or
  ring on the marker.
  Risk: Low
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ID: F8
  Problem: The map region has no accessible name.
  Proposed fix: Wrap it in a <section aria-label="Parks map">.
  Risk: Low
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ID: E1
  Problem: fitBounds on two very close parks zooms in extremely.
  Proposed fix: Pass maxZoom: 15.
  Risk: Low
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ID: E2
  Problem: Leaflet's default .leaflet-div-icon adds a white box and border to a
  divIcon.
  Proposed fix: Set className: "" and style via Tailwind.
  Risk: Low
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ID: E3
  Problem: No live regions for loading and error.
  Proposed fix: Loading uses role="status" and the error view uses role="alert".
  Risk: Low
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ID: E4
  Problem: Images could shift layout between loaded and fallback states.
  Relative
  image URLs would be dropped silently.
  Proposed fix: Fixed aspect-ratio box for every image and fallback. State
  plainly that only absolute http(s) URLs are kept.
  Risk: Low
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ID: E5
  Problem: A scrollable list inside the page would be a second scroll container,

  which axe flags unless it's focusable.
  Proposed fix: No nested scroll containers; use page scroll.
  Risk: Low

  Round 3 — code compatibility and tests

  ID: C1
  Problem: jsdom has no scrollIntoView or matchMedia, so useFocusOnSelect would
  throw in tests.
  Proposed fix: Guard both calls in the hook (feature-check before use).
  Risk: Medium
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ID: C3
  Problem: A static import "leaflet" anywhere outside ParkMap would break the
  server render (AC-014).
  Proposed fix: Add to Boundaries: Leaflet and react-leaflet imports only inside

  ParkMap; lib/ stays DOM-free.
  Risk: Medium
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ID: C2
  Problem: Vitest needs the @/* alias and the React plugin configured, and the
  API test should run in the node environment.
  Proposed fix: Note in 7.3. No extra dependency needed for the alias.
  Risk: Low
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ID: C5
  Problem: Tailwind only generates classes it finds as literal strings,
  including
  inside divIcon HTML.
  Proposed fix: Write marker classes as literal strings.
  Risk: Low
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ID: F7
  Problem: The AC-009 "deep-equals the file" test would compare the import to
  itself.
  Proposed fix: Compare the response against the file read from disk.
  Risk: Low

  Round 4 — niche (stopping)

  N1 memoize the context value and the two marker icons. N2 console.warn a count
  of dropped records in development. N3 add a DevTools "block /api/parks" step
  to the manual checklist, because the real API can't fail by itself.

  Totals: 8 flaws (F1–F8), 5 edge cases (E1–E5), 5 code-compatibility issues
  (C1–C3, C5, and the F7 test issue), 3 non-functional gaps (N1–N3).

  None of these conflicts with existing code. One conflicts with a decision you
  approved earlier, so I need your call on it.

      254  ### 7.3 Test Data and Determinism
      255 -A fixture builder in `src/lib/fixtures/` clones sample records
          -. Tests mock `fetch`, simulate Leaflet `tileerror`, and fire `
          -error` on images. No clocks or randomness are involved. jsdom 
          -has no layout, so viewport, bounds, contrast, and focus-ring c
          -laims are verified manually. If Leaflet proves unreliable in j
          -sdom, `ParkMap` is mocked at the explorer boundary and marker 
          -behaviour is tested in its own file; this is checked during im
          -plementation.                                                 
      255 +A fixture builder in `src/lib/fixtures/` clones sample records
          +. Tests mock `fetch`, simulate Leaflet `tileerror`, and fire `
          +error` on images. No clocks or randomness are involved. jsdom 
          +has no layout, so viewport, bounds, contrast, and focus-ring c
          +laims are verified manually. If Leaflet proves unreliable in j
          +sdom, `ParkMap` is mocked at the explorer boundary and marker 
          +behaviour is tested in its own file; this is checked during im
          +plementation. Test setup: the Vitest config maps the `@/*` ali
          +as and enables the React plugin; the API test declares the nod
          +e environment; `next/dynamic` is mocked or resolved synchronou
          +sly in component tests; jsdom lacks `scrollIntoView` and `matc
          +hMedia`, so `useFocusOnSelect` feature-checks them and tests s
          +tub them where they assert scrolling. Marker keyboard activati
          +on (Enter and Space) is verified against the installed Leaflet
          + 1.9.4, because the reachable Leaflet docs describe a newer br
          +anch.

      289  ### 9.2 Initial map view (DES-026)
      290  - **0 marker parks:** world view, `center [20, 0]`, zoom 2.
      291  - **1 marker park, or ≥ 2 with all-identical coordinates:** ce
           ntre on it at zoom 14 (identical points would give zero-area b
           ounds).
      292 -- **≥ 2 distinct points:** bounds of min/max lat and lng, padd
          -ing `[32, 32]` so edge markers are not clipped.               
      292 +- **≥ 2 distinct points:** bounds of min/max lat and lng, padd
          +ing `[32, 32]` so edge markers are not clipped, and `maxZoom: 
          +15` so two very close parks do not zoom in extremely.

      296  ### 9.3 Focus on selection (DES-009)
      297 -Selection increments `focusRequest`. The effect runs only when
          - `focusRequest > 0` (never on first load, Q-007): it focuses t
          -he details heading, then scrolls it into view with `behavior: 
          -"smooth"`, or `"auto"` under `prefers-reduced-motion`.        
      297 +Selection increments `focusRequest`. The effect runs only when
          + `focusRequest > 0` (never on first load, Q-007): it focuses t
          +he details heading, then scrolls it into view only if it is no
          +t already fully inside the viewport, with `behavior: "smooth"`
          +, or `"auto"` under `prefers-reduced-motion`. `scrollIntoView`
          + and `matchMedia` are feature-checked before use (jsdom lacks 
          +both).                                                        
      298  
      299  *Assessment:* a counter, not the selected ID, drives the effec
           t, so re-activating the same park refocuses. Pulling focus bac
           k after the user moved it is the intended behaviour (Q-006).

  File Reviewed

  - docs/design/design_spec_parks-explorer-2026-10-02.md

  Rounds Completed

  - 4 of 3–5. Round 4 turned up only niche items, so I stopped there.

  Issues Found

  - Flaws: 8 (F1–F8)
  - Edge cases: 5 (E1–E5)
  - Code compatibility issues: 5 (C1–C3, C5, and the F7 test issue)
  - Non-functional gaps: 3 (N1–N3)

  Improvements Applied

  All 21 were written into the SDD in place, with structure and section
  numbering unchanged. The ones that matter most:
  - Desktop layout (F2): the list is on the left, and the map sits above the
    details on the right. DOM order is list, map, details everywhere, so tab
    order matches reading order. This replaces the layout you approved earlier,
    and DES-027 records why.
  - Markers (F4): each marker gets a unique title plus visually hidden park-name
    text. Enter and Space activation is to be verified on Leaflet 1.9.4, with a
    keydown fallback for Space if needed. The reachable Leaflet docs describe a
    newer branch, so I couldn't confirm this myself.
  - Fetch races (F1): the fetch hook aborts on cleanup and ignores stale
    responses.
  - Page shell (F3): one <h1>, <main>, labelled sections, and real page metadata
    in place of the scaffold's "Create Next App".
  - jsdom (C1): the focus hook feature-checks scrollIntoView and matchMedia,
    which jsdom lacks and which would crash tests.
  - Boundaries (C3): Leaflet imports are only allowed inside ParkMap, which
    protects the server render (AC-014).

  The rest of the list covers smaller items: scroll only when the heading isn't
  already visible, a non-colour selected cue, maxZoom: 15, loading and error
  live regions, the API test reading the file from disk, memoization, a dev-only
  warning for dropped records, and a manual "block /api/parks" step.

  Open Questions

  None.

  Human Quality Gate

  - Status: Pending. The layout change is a material edit, so the gate was
    reset. The review record is in the SDD's gate section.
  - Evidence: your answers in conversation, which accepted all findings and
    chose the layout option.

  - Expected authored LOC: production about 650–1,010, tests about 1,100–1,815,

    it. Nothing is near the 1,000-line limit.

    - Phase 3 covers network failure and timeouts.

      at its start. That is outside Phases 2–4, and the plan explains why.

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Yes. Reading the plan's actual dependencies and file lists, five tasks can run
  at once right now, and the plan itself only marks two of them as
  parallel-safe.

  Parallel waves, by real dependencies

  Wave: 1 (ready now)
  Phases: P6 ParkMap, P7 ParksContext, P8 useFocusOnSelect, P9 format helpers,
  P12 API route
  Why they're independent: Every dependency is already done, and no two touch
  the
  same file. Only P6 edits package.json.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Wave: 2
  Phases: P10 ParkList, P11 ParkDetails, P13 ExplorerLayout
  Why they're independent: They need P6 only for the installed jest-dom and
  user-event packages, and P11 also needs P9. They share no files.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Wave: 3–7
  Phases: P14, P15, P16, P17, P18
  Why they're independent: A true chain: each needs the one before.

  The plan's own parallel list is narrower: it blesses only P9 and P12 (P3 and
  P5 are done). Its "must be sequential" line chains P6 → P7, but P7 depends
  only on P2 and P4, so that line is overly cautious. P8, P10, P11 and P13 are
  also independent.

  What stops it from just working

  1. Checkpoint gates. The plan puts approval gates after Phases 4–6, 7–8 and
     9–10, and the rule is that no phase after a gate starts until it's
     approved. So the plan's gates, not the data, currently force a strict
     sequence. Running Wave 1 in parallel means regrouping the checkpoints. That
     is a material edit to the remaining plan, so you'd need to re-approve it.
  2. RESULT-ID collisions. Each phase picks the next unused RESULT-* number.
     Parallel workers would all pick RESULT-037, so each would need a
     pre-assigned range.
  3. Merge conflicts. Every phase edits the plan file (its evidence block and
     the adjacent checkbox lines) and appends rows to
     docs/traceability/index.md. Merging parallel branches will conflict there.
     The conflicts are mechanical but need resolving each time.
  4. A shared test run. Each worktree only runs its own tests, so a full-suite
     run on the merged branch is needed after each wave.
  5. P6 may change the plan. Its jsdom feasibility check could trigger the
     fallback to a stubbed map. That only affects P14 and P15, which come later,
     so it doesn't block Wave 1.

  My recommendation

  Run Wave 1 as five parallel workers, each in its own worktree. Patch the plan
  first so it has:
  - a regrouped checkpoint after the whole wave,
  - explicit RESULT-ID ranges,
  - the corrected parallelization table.

  Then run Wave 2 in parallel once P6 and P9 have merged. By my rough guess this
  saves around 25–35 minutes of agent time. The saving is about the combined
  time of the small phases, because P6 dominates the wave. The cost is the plan
  patch, your re-approval, and the merge handling. If you'd rather keep it
  simple, staying sequential costs nothing and keeps the current approvals.

  I checked each worker's branch myself, not just from its report: commit
  contents, test counts, tsc and lint where relevant. I then merged them in
  phase order. The only conflicts were the ones the protocol predicted, adjacent
  TODO checkboxes and appended traceability rows. I resolved those with a
  script that aborts on anything else.

  Verification on the merged branch (2422d0b)

  - Tests: 10 files, 156 passing, which is exactly 84 + 17 + 9 + 15 + 26 + 5.
  - Other checks: lint, tsc, build and npm audit all exit 0, with 0
    vulnerabilities. The build lists /api/parks.
  - Evidence integrity: no duplicate RESULT IDs, every worker stayed inside its
    reserved range, and there are no conflict markers.

  The merged run is recorded as RESULT-150 to RESULT-156.

  Findings for you at Checkpoint 2

  1. Phase 6 answered the two Leaflet risks.
     - Enter and Space (R-002): Leaflet 1.9.4 does not activate a marker on
       Enter or Space. The SDD-approved keydown fallback is implemented, and it
       also stops Space from scrolling the page.
     - jsdom (R-003): Leaflet does run in jsdom, so the "stub the map" fallback
       isn't needed.
  2. A deliberate deviation in Phase 6. Marker icons are built per park and
     memoized, not two module-level icons. The design requires hidden park-name
     text in each marker. Names are untrusted, so the icon is built from a DOM
     text node, not an HTML string, and a hostile-name test covers it.
  3. A decision for you, from Phase 7. retry doesn't clear the selection. If a
     failed load is retried and returns the same data, the earlier selection
     comes back. The plan and design are silent on this, and clearing it is a
     one-line change.
  4. Phase 8 ignores any focus request already present at mount, so a remount
     can't steal focus. It also focuses with preventScroll, so the hook alone
     decides whether to scroll.
  5. A reminder from Phase 7. Vitest doesn't type-check, and a test type error
     was caught only by tsc and build. I've been running both in every phase.
  6. Most phases ran over their LOC estimates, mostly from extra test cases (for
     example Phase 8: 198 against 85–125). Each stayed under the 400-line
     limit.

  Next

  Please review and say whether you approve Checkpoint 2, and tell me your call
  on the retry-selection point (item 3). Once approved, Wave 2 starts: Phases
  10, 11 and 13, in parallel with the same setup.

    duplicate RESULT IDs and no conflict markers.

  The merged run is recorded as RESULT-220 to RESULT-226.

  Findings for you at Checkpoint 3

  1. A defect the tests caught in Phase 11. Repeated amenities or repeated image
     URLs triggered React's duplicate-key warning. The data is untrusted and
     nothing dedupes those two lists, so the keys now include the item's
     position.
  2. Focus visibility in high-contrast mode (Phase 10). The worker added
     focus-visible:outline-hidden, which isn't in the plan. It keeps the focus
     indicator visible when Windows high-contrast mode drops box-shadow rings.
  3. The 1024 px breakpoint was checked in the built CSS (Phase 13). lg resolves
     to min-width:64rem, so 375 px and 1280 px fall on opposite sides, as the
     acceptance criteria assume.
  4. New "not provided" labels (Phase 11). "Hours not provided" and "Acreage not
     provided" extend the plan's label assumption A8.
  5. Import styles.
     - Components (ParkMap, ParkList, ParkDetails, ParkImage) use default
       exports.
     - ExplorerLayout is a named export, matching its folder.
     - Phase 14 will import each as it is. It must also pass headingRef into
       ParkDetails.
  6. Size. Phases 10, 11 and 13 ran over their LOC estimates (178 vs 100–140,
     347 vs 205–300, 106 vs 75–110), mostly from extra test cases. All stay
     under the 400-line limit.
  7. Still open: the Phase 7 question of whether retry should clear the
     selection.

  Phase 15 — map wiring and focus handoff (b97ddbe)

  - What it does: the Leaflet map loads client-only after the data is ready.
    Selecting a park from the list or a marker highlights both, shows the
    details and focuses the details heading. Loading the page changes neither
    focus nor scroll.
  - Tests: 21 across AC-005, 007, 008, 012, 013, 015 and 003.
  - Whole suite: 15 files and 240 tests passing (219 + 21). Lint, build, tsc and
    audit all exit 0, with no React warnings.

  What went wrong and how it was resolved

  1. I broke Phase 14's tests. The map's markers are also buttons named after
     each park, so Phase 14's global queries matched two elements (12 failures).
     Fixing it meant editing a file outside Phase 15's allowed list, so I
     stopped and asked. You chose to stub the map in those tests: one mock, 6
     lines added, 0 removed, no assertion touched. I recorded it as a plan
     revision.
  2. vitest-axe's matcher types don't fit Vitest 5. This broke tsc and build,
     even though the tests ran. The package itself works, so the tests assert on
     results.violations directly.
  3. Two mutations survived. Nothing checked that the map stays out until the
     data is ready. I added two tests and both mutations are now caught. My
     first attempt at three of the mutations didn't apply because of a shell
     quoting bug, and I discarded those results and redid them.

  One finding for Phase 18

  Neither removing ssr: false nor replacing the dynamic import with a plain
  static import breaks the production build. The server render never includes
  the map, because it only renders after the client fetch finishes. So nothing
  automated guards ssr: false or the code-split chunk. I recommend extending
  Phase 18's guard test to require ssr: false and no static ParkMap import in
  ParkExplorer.tsx. That changes the remaining plan text, so it needs your
  decision.

  Other things to know

  - vitest-axe pulls in axe-core under the MPL-2.0 licence. It's dev-only and
    nothing ships, but the plan's dependency table only listed the wrapper's MIT
    licence. The package was last released in January 2025.
  - Phase 15 ran about 24 LOC over its estimate (328 vs 192–304), under the
    400-line limit.
  - The Phase 7 question of whether retry should clear the selection is still
    open.

  - [ ] src/components/ParkMap.tsx:73 — event as LeafletKeyboardEvent is an
    unchecked cast, needed because Leaflet's handler types don't narrow keydown.
  - [ ] vitest.config.ts — the Vite ESM warning on every npm test is still there
    (rename to .mts or set "type": "module").
  - [ ] AGENTS.md — "Integration tests" and "Types" still say N/A. The ">3
    files" planning rule conflicts with the plan's up-to-4-file phases.
  - [ ] src/components/ParkExplorer.test.tsx:21 — the next/dynamic stub means
    real chunk loading and the loading placeholder are untested. The TEST-055
    text guard and manual TEST-047 cover this.

  What's Done Well

  - normalizeParks (src/lib/parks.ts) is a strict untrusted-data boundary. It
    handles bad coordinates, duplicate ids, non-http URLs and non-array bodies,
    and never throws.
  - Markers are built from DOM nodes with textContent, so park names can't
    inject markup (src/components/ParkMap.tsx:36-50).
  - useFocusOnSelect ignores a request already present at mount, so load changes
    neither focus nor scroll. useParksFetch cancels superseded requests with an
    abort signal.
  - Every one of the 180 test titles carries a TEST-* ID. All CHG-001 to 031 are
    in the index, every RESULT cited in the index exists in the plan, and no
    RESULT ID is defined twice. Every phase has a recorded mutation check, plus
    the real-repo guard mutations in Phase 18.

    012, 015 are verified in tests. AC-002, 005 (focus ring), 006, 013

  - Performance issues found: None. The list is 12 items, marker icons are

  - Traceability complete: Mostly. Gap: the manual TEST-045 to 050 aren't listed
